D-533 PR 4a D-3: the new module owns a copy of rotation and catalog chain resolution

When
2026-11-15, #221
Context
CatalogTrust exposes only prepare_installation, evaluate and resolve_route; its rotation and catalog resolution is private and excluded.
Options
(a) take already-verified records as input; (b) call evaluate; (c) own a copy moved from the authoring mirror.
Decision
Own a copy, taking normalized rotation and catalog envelopes. Add an equivalence test against CatalogTrust.evaluate on rotation-only evidence. 4c deletes the duplicate in catalog_trust.
Reason
Replacement and rotation nodes are mutually dependent (a rotation can have a replacement parent and the reverse), so the graph cannot be built from pre-resolved rotation nodes. The equivalence test limits drift until 4c.

D-534 PR 4a D-4: step 5 split for order independence

When
2026-11-15, #221
Context
Step 5 compares the first catalog's inventory and entries, but that catalog may arrive after the replacement (#211 post-cutoff P2, order-independence, D-279).
Options
Keep it in node eligibility; move it to catalog validation under the node.
Decision
Node eligibility keeps only claim-intrinsic checks (parents known, prior_catalogs structure, generation, service rule). The inventory digest match and the entries-extend-priors check run when the first catalog under the node is validated, as the existing previous: nil rotation check does. A bad first catalog is refused as a catalog (invalid_catalog_transition); the node is unchanged.
Reason
Eligibility must be a function of the candidate and its closure only. The cost is that a node whose digest matches no valid catalog can never be promoted; an admin resolution cites it, as with any head. This deviates from the plan text and needs rule 3 review.

D-535 PR 4a D-5: unknown parent is pending, ineligible parent is a transition failure (row 350)

When
2026-11-15, #221
Context
The interim code returns invalid_catalog_transition for an unknown parent, which depends on whether the parent rotation has arrived.
Options
(a) invalid_catalog_transition; (b) raw evidence; (c) pending.
Decision
A parent id that is not yet a held rotation or a delivered replacement op makes the candidate pending (trust_pending): no node, no head, no closure. A parent that is a known ineligible candidate gives invalid_catalog_transition. Intrinsic failures are raw evidence per OD-17 and no longer block authoring. The preflight still refuses trust_pending for a Core-honored pending candidate so the operator supplies the evidence.
Reason
Pending resolves monotonically as evidence arrives, so the verdict never flips. A permanently unprovable parent id can still pause authoring; that residual is recorded, not hidden.

D-536 PR 4a D-6: input and state shape

When
2026-11-15, #221
Context
The accepted state is a watermark plus a sticky block that catalog_trust persists; verdicts must not become persisted order-dependent state.
Options
Persist node verdicts; or recompute from the evidence union each call.
Decision
The judgement is a pure function of the evidence union plus the prior accepted watermark. Intrinsic verdicts are recomputed, nothing new is persisted, and the block is recomputed each call. The only order-dependent output is authority_changed versus a plain refusal. Stickiness stays in catalog_trust.
Reason
This matches how catalog_trust already rebuilds its graph from state.histories, rotations and catalogs, and it keeps restore of pre-upgrade stores a non-issue on the graph side.

D-537 PR 4a D-7: replacement nodes store generation, service pair and origin (#211 P2 item 2)

When
2026-11-15, #221
Context
Bootstrap and rotation nodes carry generation; entry service checks and route origin read context.bootstrap today.
Options
Read the bootstrap; store per node.
Decision
Every node stores generation, key, service_id, service_key, origin, parents, prior, inventory, refused? and pending. Entry and route checks must read the current node (used by 4c).
Reason
A replacement changes service identity and origin; reading the bootstrap would dial the lost service.

D-538 PR 4a D-8: beacon evidence from analyze_with_records on the strict prefix (#211 P2 item 3)

When
2026-11-15, #221
Context
Step 4 needs both acquisition records and beacons from one pass.
Options
analyze_with_beacon_evidence plus acquisitions; analyze_with_records once.
Decision
Use Authority.analyze_with_records(Space, prefix) once per candidate prefix, memoized by prefix frontier. TS uses analyzeAuthority on the prefix frames (validBeacons, acquiresByRole, delegations from op evidence).
Reason
One pass keeps the epoch rule and the acquisition on the same causal position. Cost is bounded by existing evidence bounds; no new cap.

D-539 PR 4a D-9: root-attenuated means issuer == space_root (row 356)

When
2026-11-15, #221
Context
Inherited provenance starts from the latest root-attenuated record; the PR 2 preflight read that as issuer == space_root.
Options
Adopt the PR 2 reading; follow parent chains to the root.
Decision
Adopt issuer == space_root, over the whole :admin acquisition sequence from the latest such record, with an unreadable delegation refusing. The preflight now calls the same function, so the two cannot differ.
Reason
Core already owns attenuation. The resolver-specific predicate is only ceiling monotonicity, and a continuation record is self-issued so it is later in the sequence, never the root.

D-540 PR 4a D-10: closing rule at catalog level and for ancestor bindings

When
2026-11-15, #221
Context
D-275 says a catalog 'past the cited prior_catalogs' is refused, while step 7 says an omitted retained catalog head stays catalog_fork. The plan does not say what is cited for an ancestor binding such as B in B->T->R.
Options
Treat all uncovered catalogs as closed-out; treat all as forks; split by relation to the cited catalog.
Decision
Under a closed binding: ancestor-or-self of a cited catalog is covered; a strict descendant of a cited catalog, or any catalog when the binding was cited with nil, is closed-out (catalog_rollback); a sibling of the cited catalog is an omitted head and stays catalog_fork. For an ancestor binding, the rotation's prior_catalog is the citation.
Reason
This satisfies both the closing-rule tests (C0 under {P,nil} is refused in either order) and the C12 one-sided-citation test. It fills a plan gap, so it is flagged for rule 3 review and needs no new operator question.

D-541 PR 4a D-11: accepted state advance, refused head and row 320

When
2026-11-15, #221
Context
The contract does not say what a client does when the only non-closed head is a refused node, or how authority_changed clears.
Options
Block on a refused head; hold the watermark.
Decision
A sole refused head holds the watermark with reason catalog_authority_refused and no block. authority_changed applies only when the accepted binding or catalog is refused or closed-out. It clears when a sole honored head with a ready first catalog exists, which advances the watermark (a fresh admin resolution citing the current heads, row 320).
Reason
A never-accepted refused node authorizes nothing, so it must not freeze routes the client already trusted. Reclassification of something accepted is the case the contract names for authority_changed.

D-542 PR 4a D-12: nonce-reuse fork applies among non-closed nodes

When
2026-11-15, #221
Context
Two eligible nodes with one nonce and different claims are catalog_fork, but a resolution citing both would otherwise leave a permanent fork.
Options
All eligible nodes; heads only.
Decision
Only non-closed (head) nodes count, so a covering resolution ends the fork.
Reason
Order independent (heads are a function of the union) and consistent with the C12 resolution test.

D-543 PR 4a D-13: verdict tri-state and raw evidence

When
2026-11-15, #221
Context
OD-17 makes a union-refused replacement a node only if steps 1, 2, 4-6 pass.
Options
Two states; three states.
Decision
eligible (refused? from step 3), ineligible (raw evidence, no head or closure effect, kept for export) and pending. The graph returns raw_evidence and pending lists. Reasons are only members of the closed CatalogTrust.reason set; the step-4 predicate name is a graph-local detail that 4c maps.
Reason
Pending is needed for order independence; keeping the detail graph-local avoids changing the closed detail field set.

D-544 PR 4a D-14: rollback check against the watermark

When
2026-11-15, #221
Context
catalog_rollback is in the reason set but CatalogTrust never emits it today.
Options
Leave unused; emit when the promoted head is below the watermark.
Decision
Emit catalog_rollback when the promotion head's generation, or its catalog revision under the same binding, is below the watermark, and for closed-out children (D-10). Same bytes remain idempotent.
Reason
Plan section 7 C14 requires 'a rollback is refused'; this is the narrowest rule that does so.

D-546 PR 4a D-16: vectors, CI wiring and dist

When
2026-11-15, #221
Context
replacement_v1.json and ts_trust.json are protected; CI runs catalog:trust and a vector regeneration diff.
Options

Decision

D-553 PR 4a D-A6 (critique): accept finding 6, pending is per-candidate; guard against attacker-forced trust_pending

When
2026-11-15, #221
Decision
A held-but-pending parent makes the child pending. A pending candidate holds the graph trust_pending only if it is not already refuted by an intrinsic prefix-only failure (step 4 authority); otherwise it is raw evidence. Tests for both sides.
Reason
Without the guard an unauthorized candidate with a dangling dep could pin the whole graph at rank 3 indefinitely. Authority refusal needs only the strict prefix so it is safe to evaluate before closure completes, preserving monotonicity because a prefix-refused candidate never flips.

The behavior is route policy that lives in the excluded catalog_trust routes/1, so 4a cannot decide it; but the graph can guarantee no new old-signer material is accepted, which removes the fallback risk. Rejected: forcing a block, since OD-17 and the plan's contract say no block for a refused sole head.

D-559 PR 4a D-A1 (build): early refutation of incomplete-closure candidates

When
2026-11-15, #221
Context
Plan section 3, pending scope (finding 6).
Options
Run the check on the held prefix (rejected: not monotone); pend everything (rejected: lets junk ops block the graph).
Decision
A candidate with missing deps is refuted only by facts no missing ancestor can change: its shape and scope, its own author/cap/deps bindings, its possession signatures, and the held holder_epoch op's own delegation under claim.cap (audience, replace_catalog_v1, live false, finite lease). Otherwise it stays pending and the resolve returns trust_pending.
Reason
Running the step-4 check on a partial prefix misreads the continuation profile (the spike returned :error), so an authorized candidate would be refused as wrong_catalog_scope and flip later, which breaks D-279.

D-561 PR 4a D-A4 (build): catalog and rotation failures are retained refusals; the preflight stays fail-closed

When
2026-11-15, #221
Decision
A bad catalog or rotation is recorded as a refusal and never aborts resolve. resolve errors only for malformed input, control_history_limit and trust_pending. The authoring preflight still refuses, highest precedence first, on any retained artifact refused on its own bytes, except refusals inherited from a refused node.
Reason
This makes the plan's 'bad first catalog is refused as a catalog and the node is unchanged' case work, and keeps PR 2's promise that nothing is signed over evidence Stage B rejects.

D-562 PR 4a D-A5 (build): authority_changed triggers and the row 320 clear

When
2026-11-15, #221
Decision
The block names the head at first block time, and a standing block keeps those triggers. A later sole, honored replacement head that is not a trigger and closes every trigger clears it (clears_authority_changed). A refused accepted binding is also resolved in the same delivery by an honored replacement that closes it and cites a descendant of the accepted catalog.
Reason
This prevents a permanent freeze when the resolution arrives together with the revoke, or before its first catalog. A resolution that itself closes out the accepted catalog (R citing {P, nil} after C0) still blocks.

D-563 PR 4a D-A6 (build): holder predicate and C13 fixtures

Reason
Observed: a revoke does not move holder_epochs, and a root re-transfer after a revoke is refused as revoked_capability.

D-564 PR 4a D-A9 (build): Core refusal on the candidate's own closure is intrinsic

When
2026-11-15, #221
Decision
If Core refuses the op on its strict prefix plus itself, the candidate gets catalog_authority_refused (predicate core_refused_at_prefix). Stage A's own application_invalid_catalog is excluded, because the literal and possession checks report those failures with their own reasons.
Reason
Otherwise a replacement authored after a revoke already in its prefix became an eligible refused node that closed its parents, contrary to C11/C14. This uses only the public Authority.analyze/2; authority.ex is unchanged.

D-565 PR 4a D-A7 (build): cover_claim order

When
2026-11-15, #221
Decision
Order: trust_pending (unheld parent or prior), then invalid_catalog_transition (raw, refused or closed-out parent; generation; service), then catalog_fork (omitted head or catalog head; citing a non-head ancestor counts as a sibling), then a prior that is not a catalog head, then first-catalog extension.
Reason
This keeps PR 2's existing fork expectations and matches the plan's 'unheld prior, then transition failure, then fork' wording.

D-570 PR 4a D-A12: catalog validity is judged before closed-binding classification

When
2026-11-15, #221
Context
Catalogs under a refused node keep catalog_authority_refused; existing expectations are unchanged.
Decision
Two passes: intrinsic validity (including every cited prior of any node kind, rotations included), then current/covered/closed_out/uncovered/refused. A catalog on a cited chain must have its whole previous chain held, or the resolve is trust_pending. An uncited old-key catalog with an unheld previous is closed out, never pending (pinned).
Reason
A citation must not rehabilitate an invalid catalog. Limiting pending to cited chains stops an old-key holder from stalling a closed binding.

D-571 PR 4a: authoring refuses {R1, nil} after R1 is union-refused

When
2026-11-15, #221
Decision
cover_claim expects the citable catalog heads, so omitting chain-valid K1 under refused R1 is catalog_fork.
Reason
Citing nil closes K1 out, and a resolution that closes out the accepted catalog still blocks (D-A5). Citing {R1,K1} is the claim that clears it.

D-572 PR 4a: any incomplete non-candidate op makes the resolve trust_pending

When
2026-11-15, #221
Decision
Every held Space op with an incomplete closure pends the resolve, not just catalog controls. The only exception is a candidate early-refuted from its own bytes.
Reason
Plan 316 step 2 and row m: dependency closure exact, else trust_pending. This fails closed, consistent with the 'missing control or history page' rule.

D-573 PR 4a: missing accepted artifacts fail closed on the sole-head path, not as trust_pending

When
2026-11-15, #221
Decision
When accepted is non-nil, :missing binding or catalog status is an authority_changed trigger.
Reason
A trust_pending check in run/1 would have flipped the catalog_fork scenario's step 3, which legitimately omits the earlier accepted R/KR. The trigger is cheap and fails closed.

D-576 PR 4a D-A15: refusal reason for replacement cutoff/entry mismatch (needs operator confirmation)

When
2026-11-15, #221
Context
Review finding 2 (P1). Recorded as D-A15 in plan 316.
Options
Chosen: refuse KR with invalid_catalog_transition. R can never be ready, and the result looks the same as 'first catalog not delivered yet'. Alternative: KR stays a valid catalog head and the decision is recovery_incomplete, so the next resolution must cite and extend KR. Plan row l's wording ('Incomplete or wrong cutoff ... recovery_incomplete') can be read as favouring the alternative.
Decision
When a replacement's cutoff replicas differ from its first catalog's entry replicas, that first catalog is refused with invalid_catalog_transition. The replacement never becomes ready, and the decision is head R with no reason.
Reason
The rotation precedent at catalog_trust.ex:681 already refuses a replica mismatch with invalid_catalog_transition. The inventory is fixed by the claim, so the mismatch is permanent. Plan row l's recovery_incomplete covers unproven or wrong proofs, which can still be cured.

D-577 PR 4a D-A16: D-275 non-stall rule for pending rotations

When
2026-11-15, #221
Context
Review finding 1 (P1).
Decision
A pending rotation whose rotation-parent chain reaches a closed or closed-out binding is closed-out evidence, and so is every catalog bound to it. Neither is ever trust_pending. A pending rotation under an open, unknown or pending replacement parent still pends.
Reason
Without this, an old-key signer can withhold a prior catalog and freeze recovery forever. Under D-275 the outcome is fixed whatever the missing catalog says.

F4 is the second catalog-validity-versus-classification gap, so it is deferred to a design row and not patched. The D-A15 two-step residual joins that row (filed as row 382).
Reason
The task says to stop special-casing a class once it repeats across rounds.

D-581 PR 4a D-A17: refused-parent coverage

D-582 PR 4a D-A18: only a chain-valid catalog is an omitted head

When
2026-11-15, #221
Context
Finding 2.
Decision
Under a closed binding, an uncited catalog that fails its own chain is refused as invalid_catalog_transition, and an uncited catalog whose chain is not held is closed-out evidence; neither forks.
Reason
This applies D-A12/D-A16 to catalogs, so an old-key signer cannot fork or stall recovery with artifacts that fail their own chain. A chain-valid omitted old-key catalog still forks; that residual is filed as a design question (row 384).

D-583 PR 4a D-A19: whole-cutoff proofs, an input-contract change

When
2026-11-15, #221
Context
Finding 5. The binding-graph vectors now carry frontier in every proof.
Decision
proven_cutoffs / provenCutoffs entries must be the complete {replica, frontier, log_digest}, with every frontier element a string. They are matched against a node's cutoffs whole, and any other shape is malformed_catalog.
Reason
This matches CatalogTrust, which compares the whole cutoff. 4c and any future caller must pass the frontier. The current callers (both authoring preflights) pass an empty list, so they are unaffected.

D-588 PR 4a convergence design: Block is derived; the graph persists nothing

When
2026-11-15, #221
Options
Keep triggers and patch closed-out (rejected, rule 3); fully stateless with no sticky bit (rejected: a Core verdict flip could unfreeze without a resolution).
Decision
resolve/1 drops the blocked input. Block, clears_authority_changed and serving are computed on every call from the union and the accepted watermark. No head ids are stored.
Reason
Stored head-id triggers caused the row 383 freeze and its dependence on delivery order.

D-589 PR 4a convergence design: One sticky bit plus the watermark; advance/2 is the 4c merge rule

When
2026-11-15, #221
Decision
The caller keeps only the accepted watermark and frozen = blocked.reason authority_changed. The graph exports a pure advance/2 (TS advanceCatalogBindingState) that 4c's merge_security_block adopts. Derived blocks are recomputed each call and never kept.
Reason
This gives a single place where the freeze lifts. Today's merge_security_block(previous, nil) would make every derived block sticky.

D-590 PR 4a convergence design: Clearing rule

When
2026-11-15, #221
Decision
frozen lifts only when the decision accepts a sole promotable head H, an honored replacement in H's lineage lies outside the watermark binding's ancestry, H's catalog carries the accepted catalog's entries and routes, and the accepted catalog was either cited in H's lineage or closed out by a node that a later replacement descends from.
Reason
This is row 320 stated over the watermark and the graph. It keeps D-A5's rule that a resolution which itself closes out the accepted catalog still blocks.

D-591 PR 4a convergence design: Freshness is not stored

When
2026-11-15, #221
Decision
A replacement that existed before the freeze and later becomes promotable may clear it.
Reason
Core's verdict on the full union decides authority. Recording the head at block time was the freeze.

D-592 PR 4a convergence design: Generation rollback replaced by ancestry

When
2026-11-15, #221
Decision
Rollback is a lower catalog revision under the same binding. The generation comparison is removed.
Reason
With a sole head and a held watermark binding, the head cannot be an ancestor of the watermark. The generation check blocked a closed-out rotation watermark at a higher generation.

D-593 PR 4a convergence design: Promotion carries the watermark inventory

When
2026-11-15, #221
Decision
Any promotion away from the accepted binding needs the new catalog to extend the accepted catalog's entries and keep its routes.
Reason
Property (d): no accepted sequence drops a retained entry, including watermarks that a resolution closed out.

D-594 PR 4a convergence design: A replacement citing an invalid prior is raw evidence

When
2026-11-15, #221
Options
Keep R as a node with C0 uncovered (D-A12, rejected: it forces an extra resolution for an admin mistake).
Decision
Step 5 requires every non-nil cited prior to be intrinsically valid, otherwise invalid_catalog_transition (predicate prior). This replaces D-A12's 'covers nothing, fork' reading.
Reason
Row 382 asks for R and KR to be refused. It is symmetric with rotations and stays intrinsic, so D-279 holds.

D-595 PR 4a convergence design: Valid means intrinsically valid

When
2026-11-15, #221
Decision
Catalog validity never reads the union refused? flag of its binding node.
Reason
The C13 citation of K1 under refused R1 must stay a valid prior, and D-279 must not depend on the union.

D-596 PR 4a convergence design: Rotation eligibility reads prior validity

When
2026-11-15, #221
Decision
A rotation becomes a node only when its prior catalog is held and valid. An invalid prior makes the rotation a retained refusal, not a head.
Reason
Open P2 at ex:308 / TS:240. The pinned rotation_refused_prior expectation flips.

D-597 PR 4a convergence design: A nil citation carries the parent's floor

When
2026-11-15, #221
Decision
Floor(N) is the union of the cited catalogs' entries, or the parent's floor where the citation is nil. Every first catalog must extend its binding's floor.
Reason
Open P1 at ex:737 / TS:473. Inventory obligations now survive a parent whose first catalog was refused.

D-598 PR 4a convergence design: Route uniqueness is judged against a catalog's own ancestry

When
2026-11-15, #221
Decision
route_history and the union id-order reservation are deleted. Routes are checked against the previous chain and the cited priors' route maps, recursively. This replaces D-A10. In 4c, validate_route_history! over all catalogs is replaced by this check.
Reason
Open P2 at ex:859 / TS:525. Union id-order refusal was not monotone. Only the head's catalog is served, and its ancestry is checked.

D-599 PR 4a convergence design: Union-refused sole head freezes routes

When
2026-11-15, #221
Decision
When an eligible replacement has closed the accepted binding and the sole head is refused, the decision carries a derived catalog_authority_refused block and serving nil.
Reason
Today no block is emitted, and CatalogTrust.routes/1 would serve the closed old binding. The operator's rule forbids that fallback.

D-600 PR 4a convergence design: Honored but not ready replacement also freezes routes

When
2026-11-15, #221
Decision
An honored replacement head that closes the accepted binding but is not yet promotable gives serving nil with no block.
Reason
This is the same old-signer fallback. It goes beyond item 3 and D-568 question 1, so it is flagged for operator confirmation.

D-601 PR 4a convergence design: Planned rotation keeps serving

When
2026-11-15, #221
Decision
If only rotations lie between the accepted binding and the head, the accepted catalog stays served while the rotation is not ready.
Reason
CatalogTrust parity. The old key is still the current authority there, so this is not a fallback.

D-602 PR 4a convergence design: Fork bindings are cited as extra parents

When
2026-11-15, #221
Decision
Authoring cover_claim requires, as parents, every closed binding that holds an uncovered catalog, with that catalog as a prior. The verifier is unchanged.
Reason
Without this, a D-A17 fork needs two resolutions, and row 320 liveness fails for a single one. Lifecycle section 3 says parents are binding-head IDs, so this is flagged for the operator. Fallback: (b) excludes uncovered-under-closed forks.

D-603 PR 4a convergence design: Row 384 partly answered, rest stays open

When
2026-11-15, #221
Decision
An omitted chain-valid old-key catalog under a closed binding still forks, but one resolution that cites it and carries its entries ends the fork. Whether such post-closure siblings should be closed out stays an operator question.
Reason
Closing them out would contradict the pinned C12 one-sided-citation test. The old signer can still force one resolution per sibling.

D-604 PR 4a convergence design: Preflight head limit is a filed hole

When
2026-11-15, #221
Decision
Property (b) is scoped to histories whose resolve returns ok. A new row is proposed to report more than two heads in the decision, so a resolution can still be prepared.
Reason
The verifier accepts R* because R* cites all heads, but the preflight's resolve errors with control_history_limit before cover_claim runs.

D-605 PR 4a convergence design: Missing accepted artifacts

When
2026-11-15, #221
Decision
An accepted binding or catalog absent from the input is a derived authority_changed, and it can clear only once the input supplies it.
Reason
Clearing needs the watermark's entries. 4c's validate_watermark! guarantees they are present.

D-606 PR 4a convergence design: Decision shape changes

When
2026-11-15, #221
Decision
The decision gains serving. The block becomes {reason, ids} with informational ids. Binding-graph vectors are regenerated; historical vectors and wire bytes are unchanged.
Reason
4c needs a route-freeze signal that does not depend on stored blocks.

D-607 PR 4a convergence design: Liveness is conditional

When
2026-11-15, #221
Decision
The row 320 proof and property (b) assume an honored admin, an author holding the verifier's union and the watermark artifacts, obtainable cutoffs, consistent per-replica extended keys, an ok resolve, and no new concurrent control.
Reason
Without these preconditions the claim would be an overclaim. 4a has no entry facts.

D-608 PR 4a convergence design: Property harness

When
2026-11-15, #221
Decision
Properties draw subsets and causal orders from a pre-signed pool, enumerate every order for six artifacts or fewer, persist state through one shim that works on today's code and on advance/2, and sign R* on demand.
Reason
Signed fixtures are expensive, and the properties must run red against the current code before the change.

D-609 PR 4a convergence design: Order independence is scoped

When
2026-11-15, #221
Decision
Property (e) requires the same decision for orders that end with the same watermark, and the same end state once a resolution that carries every possible watermark is present.
Reason
A client that accepted a catalog earlier protects it (D-A5). That difference is intended.

D-610 PR 4a convergence design: Differential source and mapping

When
2026-11-15, #221
Decision
Property (f) uses TreehouseCatalogTrustVectors histories without replacements, calls CatalogTrust.evaluate read-only, maps 'no ready catalog' to trust_pending and a CatalogTrust throw to a graph refusal, and excludes entry-fact and cross-branch route-reuse histories.
Reason
CatalogTrust checks entry facts and refuses the whole page. The graph does neither, so those divergences are by design.

D-611 PR 4a convergence design: C1 rotation fork: accept as a derived union-level fork

When
2026-11-15, #221
Options
Intrinsic refusal of T (breaks property (a)); union-level fork (chosen); ignore (old-signer serving)
Decision
Add rotation forks: for each final rotation T whose parent is not closed, a catalog under T.parent that strictly descends from T.prior_catalog. A chain-valid descendant forks and a pending one pends. Fork bindings now include open rotation-fork parents, and R* cites [T, T.parent].
Reason
Verified. closed_set/1 seeds only from replacements, so B stays open and C1 current, and the graph has nothing like catalog_trust.ex:1288-1300. Making it union-level, not intrinsic, preserves D-279 monotonicity. The pending treatment mirrors CatalogTrust, which includes pending catalogs.

D-612 PR 4a convergence design: C2 serving and persisted block shape: accept as 4c obligations

When
2026-11-15, #221
Options
Persist serving as state; recompute serving (chosen)
Decision
Routes come from a recomputed serving. next.blocked holds only the sticky authority_changed and CatalogTrust's own reasons. validate_saved_block! is rewritten so derived graph blocks are valid with blocked nil, and unknown reasons refuse.
Reason
Verified. resolve_route and routes/1 read state.accepted and state.blocked, and validate_saved_block! has only three arms. catalog_trust.ex is hard-excluded from 4a, so these are recorded as 4c obligations.

D-613 PR 4a convergence design: C3 Seen over a closed-out rotation watermark: accept with a separate CatLineage

Verified that on_chain?/4 follows previous only, so K1 is not an ancestor of KT''. closed_status and C12 depend on previous-only semantics, so on_chain?/4 must not change.

D-614 PR 4a convergence design: C4 three rotation heads: accept with an authoring-only limit escape

When
2026-11-15, #221
Options
Collapse refused rotation heads (breaks C14/(f) parity); authoring escape (chosen)
Decision
The verifier's head limit is unchanged (C14 and plan lines 107/376; the (f) parity holds). On control_history_limit, cover_claim accepts a claim that cites every head root and fork binding, using expected priors from a resolve with the closed-out rotations removed. A post-sign resolve(U ∪ R*) check is also required. advance on control_history_limit keeps state and serves nil.
Reason
Verified. heads > 2 errors at :221, and under_refused pins a rotation under a refused node as a head. D-568 has no head-limit decision, so the critique is right that P5 was unbacked. The verifier already accepts R2 citing R1 through out?/3. A collapse of rotation heads at head counting would break the CatalogTrust differential.

D-615 PR 4a convergence design: C5 two-hop route freeing: accept with a cumulative served-route map

When
2026-11-15, #221
Options
Reserve every held catalog under a nil-cited binding (non-monotone, squat lever); cumulative map (chosen)
Decision
The watermark carries routes (route -> replica, grow-only). RouteCompat checks against it. R* chooses routes not used by any held catalog.
Reason
Verified: RouteFloor for a nil citation is RouteFloor(P), which is empty under bootstrap. A union-level reservation over held catalogs would be non-monotone, and it would let the old key squat K*'s routes after publication. The cumulative map protects exactly what the client served.

D-616 PR 4a convergence design: C6 refused entry target: accept as precondition P7 and an open question

When
2026-11-15, #221

No liveness is claimed when an accepted entry's creation or reference target is Core-refused. Retiring such a Thread is filed as an operator question.
Reason
Verified: security_block yields authority_changed from authority_refusals, and Carry forces the entry forward. Defining retirement changes catalog semantics beyond the settled contract.

D-617 PR 4a convergence design: C7 same-page closed-out descendant: accept as the documented D-275 trade

Keep closed_status (C12 settled). Document it, pin S14, and add property (g): entries of covered catalogs are carried, and only closed-out catalogs drop entries.
Reason
Verified at catalog_binding_graph.ex:857: a strict descendant of a cited catalog is closed_out. The difference between orders is the intended D-A5 watermark protection.

D-618 PR 4a convergence design: C8 property weakness: partly accepted; reject 4c route-path testing in 4a

When
2026-11-15, #221
Options
Accept all (impossible under the file exclusion); partial (chosen)
Decision
Add a test-side ReferenceModel oracle for R* and expected serving. Expand the pool with a rotation fork, three TRs, two-hop nil routes and, if reachable, a Core flip. Serving is tested against the oracle, not through CatalogTrust routes.
Reason
(1) and (2) are valid. (3) cannot be done in 4a because catalog_trust.ex and its TS twin are excluded, so the 4c suite carries it. Whether the fixtures can produce a Core refused-to-honored flip is unverified, so the design records the fallback instead of promising it.

D-619 PR 4a convergence design: C9 missing artifacts: accept as a non-freezing derived block

When
2026-11-15, #221
Decision
The block gains an explicit freeze flag, set only by Changed(A). Missing(A) gives authority_changed with freeze false and serving nil. advance and the harness read the persisted frozen bit, not block.reason.
Reason
Without this, a single incomplete call freezes until an unnecessary admin replacement, because clears needs X outside Anc*(A.binding).

D-620 PR 4a convergence design: C10 C13 client reading: pin the derived reading

When
2026-11-15, #221
Options
Sticky freeze for closed-under-refused (needs a new Changed condition); derived (chosen)
Decision
A = B/C0 with refused R1 citing {B, C0} is not frozen and serves nothing. A later Core honor of R1 is an ordinary first acceptance. The section 3 sentence 'frozen until R2' is withdrawn, and S5 and (c) pin the derived reading.
Reason
This is what 1.2's definitions give: B is closed, not closed out, and C0 is covered. Routes are nil under either reading, so the operator rule holds. B is never served again, so there is no old-signer fallback.

D-623 PR 4a convergence build: Pin flips beyond the design's list

The lower-generation rollback pin now accepts and is renamed 'a higher stored generation under the same binding is informational'. recovery_incomplete is a derived block that does not freeze. D-625 PR 4a convergence build: Watermark-relative Covers failures use catalog_rollback

When
2026-11-15, #221
Options
invalid_catalog_transition (rejected).
Decision
A promotable head that fails Seen, Carry or RouteCompat against an unchanged watermark gets reason catalog_rollback, with no block, in both runtimes.
Reason
Plan section 3 (D-279) reserves invalid_catalog_transition for intrinsic verdicts. A client with no watermark would accept the same union, so the failure is a union-level reason. No pinned scenario reaches this case.

D-626 PR 4a convergence build: Authoring route rule is weaker than 'disjoint'

When
2026-11-15, #221
Options
Strict disjointness plus test churn (rejected).
Decision
cover_claim refuses an entry only when some held catalog maps that route to a different replica. Reusing a route for the same replica is allowed.
Reason
Strict disjointness breaks many existing authoring tests in both runtimes, which reuse per-replica routes. The weaker rule gives the same safety, because every route a client served was in some held catalog.

D-627 PR 4a convergence build: Head-root rule only inside the limit escape

2026-11-15, #221

Normal coverage still requires citing every binding head. Roots stand in for rotation heads only in resolve_for_claim, after control_history_limit.
Reason
The design also says PR 2 authoring tests that cite a non-head ancestor without a fork stay catalog_fork. Accepting a root in the normal path would also prepare claims that a client accepted on a planned rotation would then freeze on.

D-628 PR 4a convergence build: Escape drops only directly chained rotations

When
2026-11-15, #221
Decision
The escape drops rotations whose rotation-parent chain reaches a cited parent, plus their descendants and the catalogs bound to them. Rotations hanging off strict ancestors of a cited root are kept, so the limit still applies if they push past it.
Reason
The authoring side has no graph at that point. This is a conservative subset of what out?/3 closes out.

After signing, the full retained evidence plus the signed op must resolve with the signed op as the sole head and no fork. It does not require an accepting decision.
Reason
An accepting decision is unreachable at signing time: K* is not signed yet and no cutoff proofs are held.

D-633 PR 4a convergence build: Findings 1 and 7 are a design defect in 1.3, not patched

When
2026-11-15, #221
Context
Operator rule: a changed authority freezes routes and never falls back to the old signer.
Options
(a) freeze from a derived trust_pending bit; (b) serve nil without freezing when Changed(A) is derivable; (c) keep as is (violates lifecycle section 4)
Decision
Reported both as design. advance/2 keeps trust_pending serving :unchanged.
Reason
Design 1.3 explicitly says trust_pending keeps state and serves :unchanged. Patching only pending_out? for refused parents would fix 7 and leave 1 open. Both repairs (a sticky freeze from the derivable part, or serving nil without freezing) trade D-A11's no-partial-history rationale against denial of service from a withheld page.

D-634 PR 4a convergence build: Findings 2 and 6 are a design defect in the RouteFloor rule

When
2026-11-15, #221
Options
Reserved-to-none conflicting floor routes (intrinsic, D-279-safe) versus union-level cross-branch classification as row 382 originally said
Decision
Reported as design, with a proposed reserved-to-none floor amendment. merge_floor and carries? are unchanged.
Reason
The design 1.4 liveness proof does not hold when cited priors conflict on a route, and P1-P7 do not exclude that case. The old catalog key alone can cause it.

D-635 PR 4a convergence build: Authoring route check exempts carried pairs

When
2026-11-15, #221
Context
Verifier unchanged; authoring preflight only.
Decision
held_conflict?/cover route check skips entries whose {route, replica} a cited non-nil prior already maps; everything else is still checked against every held catalog.
Reason
Design 5 says 'routes disjoint from every held catalog's routes'. Read literally, that lets a closed-out old-key Squat veto carrying the honest catalog, which contradicts D-A10 and the rule that the old key is never an input. A carried pair re-points nothing the cited prior had not already pointed. S12 and the fresh-route R* behave the same as before. This narrows design 5's wording without changing its intent.

D-636 PR 4a convergence build: Property oracle uses union classification but not decision flags

When
2026-11-15, #221
Decision
The oracle takes refused?, the closed and closed-out sets, catalog status and forks from the graph, and recomputes freeze, clears, serving, Seen and RouteCompat itself.
Reason
A full independent re-derivation of classification would duplicate the resolver. The surviving mutants were all in the decide and advance layer, and all six now fail. Comparing against graph classification still catches every decision-layer bug.

D-638 PR 4a convergence review: round 1 and dispositions

When
2026-11-15, #221
Context
After the convergence build (commits e14d27709 to c6719f3d7), Codex Sol 6.1 xhigh plus an independent review ran one round on c6719f3d7. The commit messages and scenario comments call it review round 5 of PR 4a (counting the four resolver rounds of D-587); this log calls it convergence round 1. The findings' line numbers refer to c6719f3d7.
Decision
Round 1 raised 7 P1/P2 findings and 1 P3. Fixed (4): same-service replacements inherit their parents' unproven cutoffs (P2, D-A15, new scenario in both runtimes and the vectors); a closed-out old-key catalog no longer vetoes authoring, because an entry whose exact {route, replica} pair a cited prior already maps is carried (P2, authoring preflight only); catalog entry service scope now outranks the signature failure (P3, new scenario); the convergence properties were rewritten around a test-side oracle that never reads decision flags, so all six surviving mutants now fail (P2, plus the S15 Seen-only scenario). Reported as design and not patched (4 findings, 2 root causes): a trust_pending page lifts an authority_changed freeze and keeps serving the old binding, both for an unrelated withheld dependency and for a rotation whose prior catalog is withheld under a refused, not-closed binding (P1 catalog_binding_graph.ex:139 advance/2 on trust_pending and :963 pending_out?, TS advanceCatalogBindingState :874 and closedOutPending :453; design 1.3 with D-A11; row 386); and merge_floor makes any resolution that cites route-conflicting sibling catalogs permanently unpromotable, reachable with only the revoked old key (P1 catalog_binding_graph.ex:912 merge_floor and :1640 carries?, TS mergeFloor :560 and coverCatalogReplacementClaim :962; reported twice; row 387). Clean final round: no. The four findings left open by D-587 (ex:737, :1097, :308, :859) were closed by the convergence design.
Reason
Rule 3: both open classes are defects in the design text (sections 1.3 and 2.4), not implementation slips, and each repair trades a settled rationale (D-A11, or row 382's union-level cross-branch reading) that the operator should choose. No clean review round is claimed after the round 1 fixes (98cd1ad08, 4dae91912).

D-640 Plan 316 PR 4a text moved from the plan to this log

When
2026-11-16, #221
Context
Plan files stop at 400 lines (plans/README.md rule 4); the PR 4a section had grown the plan from 420 lines on main to 676 (CodeRabbit thread on the plan file).
Decision
The PR 4a section of plan 316 §7 is moved here verbatim and the plan keeps a pointer with the current state:
**PR 4a — resolver as new pure modules (operator decision 2026-11-15, "new modules now, wire
later").** Not a STOP-list violation: 4a touches only new files plus the PR 2 authoring
preflight, adds no route switch, and calls `CatalogTrust` only from tests. OD-16 (D-275, D-278
reading), OD-17 (a union-refused replacement is a citable D-276 node only if steps 1, 2 incl.
causal honored bootstrap, and 4–6 pass) and row 320 are settled (operator sign-offs
2026-11-15). Split into two sequential slices, each landing BEAM and TS together:

- **4a-1:** `Treehouse.CatalogBindingGraph` / `treehouse_catalog_binding_graph.ts` (steps 1–3,
  5–9, closure, coverage, decision) and `Treehouse.CatalogReplacementAuthority` /
  `treehouse_catalog_replacement_authority.ts` (step 4). Scenario table
  `test/support/catalog_binding_graph_scenarios.ex` drives the ExUnit tests and
  `binding_graph_v1.json` (hand-written label-space expectations per delivery, persisted
  accepted/blocked state, both delivery orders); the TS twin reproduces every expectation and
  BEAM outcome and writes `binding_graph_ts_v1.json`, which BEAM reproduces.
- **4a-2:** the authoring preflight (`catalog_replacement_authoring.ex` / `.ts`) calls
  `resolve` + `cover_claim` and `CatalogReplacementAuthority.check`; the interim mirror is
  deleted. Authoring-only change to merged PR 2; it does not affect retained-client trust.

PR 4a decisions:

- **D-A1 (early refutation, finding 6).** A candidate with an incomplete closure is refuted only
  from facts no missing ancestor can change: its shape, scope, own literal bindings
  (author/cap/deps), possession signatures, and the held `holder_epoch` op's own delegation
  under `claim.cap` (audience, `replace_catalog_v1`, `live: false`, finite lease). Otherwise it
  stays pending and the resolve is `trust_pending`. Running step 4 on a partial prefix was
  rejected: the prefix profile is unresolvable there, so an authorized candidate would be
  misread as `wrong_catalog_scope` and flip later (D-279).
- **D-A2 (rotation chain checks kept).** The graph re-verifies rotation step, key separation,
  both signatures and prior-catalog agreement under the parent node, because a rotation under a
  replacement node has no page-level key and 4a-2 deletes the authoring mirror. The duplicated
  chain-logic equivalence test against `CatalogTrust.evaluate` moves to 4c (D-A3).
- **D-A4 (catalog and rotation failures are retained refusals).** They never abort a resolve;
  only malformed input, `control_history_limit` (more than two heads) and `trust_pending` do.
  The preflight stays fail-closed: any retained artifact refused on its own bytes (anything but
  `catalog_authority_refused` inherited from a refused node) refuses the preflight first.
- **D-A5 (block triggers and row 320).** An `authority_changed` block names the head at first
  block time and a standing block keeps its triggers, so a resolution delivered before its first
  catalog still clears on the next delivery. A later sole honored replacement head that is not a trigger and closes every trigger
  clears it (`clears_authority_changed`). A refused accepted binding is also resolved in the
  same delivery by an honored replacement head that closes it and cites a descendant of the
  accepted catalog (no freeze when the revoke and the resolution arrive together). A
  resolution that itself closes out the accepted catalog (R citing `{P, nil}` after C0) still
  blocks; only a later resolution clears it.
- **D-A6 (holder from the analysis).** Step 4's holder predicate requires both the analysis
  `holder_epochs[:admin]` and the last honored record. Observed: a revoke does not move
  `holder_epochs`, and a root re-transfer after revoking the admin is `revoked_capability`, so
  C13 fixtures use a concurrent holder change (admin to nominee, Core `stale_holder`).
- **D-A7 (cover_claim order).** Unheld parent or prior (`trust_pending`), then raw/refused/
  closed-out parent, generation and service (`invalid_catalog_transition`), then an omitted head
  or catalog head (`catalog_fork`; citing a non-head ancestor is a sibling of that head), then a
  prior that is not a catalog head and first-catalog extension.
- **D-A9 (Core refusal on the candidate's own closure).** Step 4 also refuses
  (`catalog_authority_refused`, predicate `core_refused_at_prefix`) when Core refuses the op on
  its strict prefix plus itself, excluding Stage A's own `application_invalid_catalog` (its
  literal and possession checks are re-run with their own reasons). A revoke or lapse already
  in the prefix therefore adds no head or closure (C11/C14); only a union-only refusal (C13) is a
  D-276 refused node. Read from the public `Authority.analyze/2`; no `authority.ex` change.
- **D-A10 (route reservation).** Only current and covered catalogs reserve routes, so a
  closed-out old-key catalog cannot refuse the honest head's first catalog.
- **D-A11 (incomplete history; review round 2 of 4a, the first after D-A9/D-A10).** Any held Space op whose closure is
  incomplete makes the resolve `trust_pending` (row m), not only replacement candidates; a
  candidate already refuted from its own bytes (D-A1) is exempt. A missing dependency of a
  concurrent revoke can no longer yield a judgment on a smaller history that later flips.
- **D-A12 (catalog validity before classification).** Catalog evaluation is two passes:
  intrinsic validity (scope, signature, entries, `previous` chain, and for a replacement's
  first catalog of any node every cited prior, a rotation's `prior_catalog` included, which
  must itself be valid), then classification. A
  citation never rehabilitates an invalid catalog: an invalid cited catalog covers nothing
  (its valid ancestors stay omitted catalog heads, `catalog_fork`) and a first catalog that
  extends it is refused. Only a catalog on a cited chain can pend, so an uncited old-key
  catalog cannot stall a closed binding.
- **D-A13 (rotation recovery, nonce reuse, citable heads).** A rotation inherits its parent's
  unproven cutoffs. Differing claims sharing a nonce fork across closed nodes too (sequential
  reuse) while the group has an open member; a later citation that closes every member
  resolves it. Authoring coverage reads `citable_catalog_heads` (current heads plus chain-valid
  catalogs under a union-refused node), so the C13 recovery claim citing `{R1, K1}` prepares
  and `{R1, nil}` is `catalog_fork`; `catalog_heads` (promotion, split forks) is unchanged.
- **D-A14 (absent accepted artifacts).** With an accepted state, an accepted binding or
  catalog absent from the input is an `authority_changed` trigger on the sole-head path, so a
  caller that omits it fails closed instead of promoting. The block persists until a fresh
  admin resolution lifts it, so its clearing belongs to the design row below.
- **Design row 383 (rule 3; finding 6 of review round 2; superseded by the convergence design below).** `authority_changed` clearing is
  a third patch on the same trigger class (D-A5's standing triggers caused this freeze: a
  trigger recorded as an old-key rotation head that a later resolution closes out rather than
  closes never clears, and the outcome depends on delivery order). Redesign before 4c wires
  `merge_security_block`: derive clearing from the accepted watermark plus the current graph
  (the accepted binding refused or closed and its catalog covered or closed out by a sole
  promotable admin resolution), not from persisted head ids. Acceptance: the R1/T2/TR/R2
  interleaving clears in both delivery orders, and a later R3 clears a client that froze.
  Review round 4 (finding 4) reproduced the freeze with the committed TS C13 fixtures: the block
  records TR, R2 closes TR out rather than closing it, and no later ordinary resolution can
  satisfy the trigger. Under adopted row 320 a fresh admin resolution citing the current heads
  must clear, so this row gates 4c.
- **D-A15 (cutoff obligations; review round 3, findings 2 and 3).** A node owes its own
  declared cutoffs plus those it inherits from a same-service parent: a rotation's pending set
  is its parent's plus its own unproven cutoffs (CatalogTrust parity, `catalog_trust.ex:505`).
  A replacement's cutoff replicas must equal its first catalog's entry replicas, as a
  rotation's equal its prior catalog's; a mismatch refuses that first catalog
  `invalid_catalog_transition` (the inventory is fixed by the claim, so the failure is
  permanent, unlike row l's unproven-proof `recovery_incomplete`), and the replacement never
  becomes ready.
- **D-A16 (D-275 non-stall; review round 3, finding 1).** One rule generalizing D-A12's
  uncited-catalog case: a pending rotation whose rotation-parent chain reaches a closed or
  closed-out binding, and every catalog bound to it, is closed-out evidence and never
  `trust_pending`, so an old-key signer cannot freeze recovery by withholding a prior catalog.
  A pending rotation under an open, unknown or pending replacement parent still pends (D-5).
- **Design row 382 (rule 3; review round 3, finding 4; superseded by the convergence design below).** Route
  reuse is the second catalog-validity-versus-classification gap after D-A12. Route reservation
  runs after validity, in union id order, so a route-refused catalog keeps `validity: ok`: a
  replacement first catalog citing it, and a `previous` successor of it (`extends?` ignores
  `route`), stay valid. Un-reserving dependents makes a fixpoint over the union non-monotone.
  Redesign before 4c wires the resolver: judge route uniqueness intrinsically against a
  catalog's own ancestry (its `previous` chain and cited priors), so it feeds validity, and
  keep only cross-branch reuse as union classification. Acceptance: C0 `{Space->A, Thread->B}`,
  C1 extending it with `{Space->B, Thread->C}`, R citing C1 with fresh routes is refused in both
  runtimes and C0 stays an omitted catalog head. The same row owns the D-A15 residual (probe,
  not patched): once R's first catalog KR is refused, R2 citing `{R, nil}` with a Space-only
  first catalog promotes and drops C0's Thread, because a node with no valid catalog is cited
  with `nil` and nothing carries its covered prior's entries forward. Acceptance there: R2 is
  refused or forks until it extends C0's entries. Review round 4 reopened all three (findings 3
  and 7) and added a fourth case of the same class (finding 6): a rotation whose prior catalog is
  intrinsically invalid (Cbad: revision 2, `previous` a revision-0 C0) still counts as a binding
  head, because rotation eligibility is judged before catalog validity; three such rotations
  give `control_history_limit`. The pinned "rotation naming a refused prior" scenario expects T
  as a head and changes under this row. The redesign is one causal evaluation in which node
  eligibility and catalog validity (chain, cited priors, route ancestry, nil-cited inventory
  carry-forward) read each other in causal order; acceptance adds: three rotations naming
  intrinsically invalid priors are refusals, not heads.
- **D-A17 (refused-parent coverage; review round 4, finding 1).** A chain-valid catalog under a
  union-refused node whose binding is closed is classified for coverage like any other: if the
  closing citations omit it, it is an uncovered catalog head (`catalog_fork`) and keeps its
  `catalog_authority_refused` refusal. The verifier now agrees with authoring's
  `citable_catalog_heads` (D-A13): R2 citing only `{R1, Ka}` beside sibling Kb forks; R2b citing
  both is accepted.
- **D-A18 (only a chain-valid catalog is an omitted head; finding 2).** Under a closed binding an
  uncited catalog that fails its own chain is refused `invalid_catalog_transition`, and one
  whose chain is not held is closed-out evidence (D-A16 applied to catalogs); neither forks. The
  author omits such an artifact from the preflight's retained set (D-A4 still refuses on it).
  Residual, not changed: a valid old-key catalog under a closed binding that the closing
  citations omit (for example a second revision-0 catalog) is still an omitted head and forks;
  that is D-275's coverage rule, not an invalid-artifact gap (design question row 384).
- **D-A19 (whole-cutoff proofs; finding 5).** `proven_cutoffs` entries are the complete
  `{replica, frontier, log_digest}` cutoff and match a node's cutoff only whole (CatalogTrust
  parity); an entry without `frontier` is `malformed_catalog` input. The binding-graph vectors
  carry `frontier` in each proof.
- **D-A8 (fixtures).** The 4a fixtures build their own Sim bounded Space (pin, root beacons,
  leased admin) instead of `CatalogVectors.bootstrap_history`, because the step-4 and R04 cases
  need the witnessed-beacon pin and continuation.

**PR 4a convergence design (rows 382 and 383; D-588–D-639).** This replaces D-A5's stored
triggers, D-A10's union route reservation, D-A12's "an invalid cited prior covers nothing"
reading and D-A14's sticky missing-artifact trigger. In force in both runtimes:

- *Block and clearing.* `resolve/1` takes no `blocked` input and persists nothing. The block,
  `clears_authority_changed` and the new `serving` field are recomputed on every call from the
  union and the accepted watermark (D-588). The caller keeps only the watermark (now with a
  grow-only cumulative `routes` map, D-615) and one sticky `frozen` bit, merged by the pure
  `advance/2` / `advanceCatalogBindingState`, which 4c adopts as `merge_security_block` (D-589).
  The block is `{reason, ids, freeze}`; `freeze` is set only by Changed(A), so a missing accepted
  artifact is a non-freezing derived block (D-605, D-619). `frozen` lifts only when the decision
  accepts a sole promotable head H, an honored replacement in H's lineage lies outside the
  watermark binding's ancestry, H's catalog carries the watermark's entries and routes (Seen over
  CatLineage, Carry, RouteCompat), and the watermark catalog was cited in H's lineage or closed
  out by a node a later replacement descends from (D-590, D-613). Freshness is not stored (D-591);
  rollback is a lower revision under the same binding (D-592); watermark-relative Covers failures
  are `catalog_rollback` (D-625).
- *Serving.* A union-refused sole head that closed the accepted binding is a derived
  `catalog_authority_refused` block with `serving` nil (D-599); an honored but not yet promotable
  replacement head also serves nil (D-600, operator confirmation pending, row 388); a planned
  rotation keeps serving the accepted catalog (D-601); `control_history_limit` keeps state and
  serves nil (D-614).
- *Validity.* Validity is intrinsic and never reads the union `refused?` flag (D-595). A
  replacement citing an invalid prior is raw evidence, `invalid_catalog_transition` (D-594,
  D-630). A rotation is a node only when its prior catalog is held and valid (D-596). Floor(N) is
  the union of the cited catalogs' entries, or the parent's floor for a nil citation, and every
  first catalog must extend it (D-597, D-631). Route uniqueness is judged against a catalog's own
  `previous` chain and cited priors' route maps; `route_history` and the union id-order
  reservation are deleted (D-598). Rotation forks are a derived union-level fork (D-611). A
  same-service replacement inherits its parents' unproven cutoffs (D-A15, fixed in review, D-638).
- *Authoring.* `cover_claim` requires closed fork bindings as extra parents (D-602), refuses an
  entry only when a held catalog maps its route to a different replica, exempting pairs a cited
  prior already maps (D-626, D-635), escapes `control_history_limit` only for claims citing every
  head root (D-614, D-627, D-628; this escape supersedes the head-limit row D-604 proposed, so
  none was filed), and checks after signing that the signed op is the sole head
  with no fork (D-629).
- *Evidence.* Scenarios S1–S15 (S5 has no scenario of its own; the C13 revoke-first pin carries it) in `catalog_binding_graph_scenarios.ex` drive both runtimes and
  the regenerated `binding_graph_v1.json` / `binding_graph_ts_v1.json`; the StreamData property
  file uses a pre-signed pool and a test-side oracle that recomputes freeze, clears, serving, Seen,
  Carry and RouteCompat from union classification without reading decision flags (D-608, D-636).

Still open after the convergence review (round 1, which the commits call round 5: 7 P1/P2 and 1 P3; 3 P1/P2 and the P3 fixed;
D-638): `trust_pending` lifts the freeze and keeps serving the refused binding (design 1.3 with
D-A11; P1 `advance/2` at `catalog_binding_graph.ex:139` and `pending_out?` at `:963`; row 386); conflicting cited route maps make
recovery permanently unpromotable, reachable with only the revoked old key (P1 `merge_floor` at `:912`; row 387).
Operator questions: row 388 (D-600, D-602, D-616) and the remainder of row 384 (D-603). Not
built: property (b) with an independent R* oracle, (e1), the (f) CatalogTrust differential, the
TS property twin, a Core refused-to-honored flip, S5's later-honor pin, and a post-sign negative
test (row 389). 4c obligations (D-612): serve from `serving`, rewrite `validate_saved_block!`,
replace `validate_route_history!` with the ancestry check.

Row dispositions: 352 closed in resolver and authoring (both runtimes). 356 closed in resolver
(D-9 reading). 350 closed in resolver for intrinsic failures (raw evidence); an unheld parent
stays pending by design (D-5); open in trust until 4c. 335 closed by the restored-permission
test (broad, narrowed, restored: Core honors, resolver refuses `inherited_provenance`); the
variant whose latest root-issued record is itself narrowed is not written (no Core-honored
construction found with the current fixtures; root cannot re-transfer while the admin holds). 354
partly closed: mirror deleted, resolver called; entry-fact refusal
(`CatalogEntries.observe_with_detail`) moves to 4c. 355 open (byte budgets). 360 open. Row 320:
implemented and tested in the graph; open in trust until 4c.

4c tasks: wire `catalog_trust.ex` / `.ts` to the graph (inputs per the 4a hand-off: bootstrap,
Space history, parsed rotation/catalog envelopes, `merge_proofs!` cutoffs, `state.accepted`,
`state.blocked`); change `merge_security_block` to lift `authority_changed` exactly when
`decision.clears_authority_changed`; move the chain-logic equivalence test; entry facts (354);
routes from the current binding's origin; pre-upgrade store restore. After the convergence
design, `merge_security_block` adopts `advance/2` instead of reading `clears_authority_changed`
alone (D-589), with the D-612 obligations above.

Open questions to the operator (not decided in 4a; question 1 is answered by D-599 and its
extension D-600 awaits confirmation in row 388, question 2 is partly answered by D-603): (1)
routes-freeze policy for a sole refused head: the graph holds the watermark and closes out every old-key child of the closed accepted
binding, but whether 4c keeps serving that binding's accepted catalog is a 4c decision. (2)
Residual in the step-7 "omitted head" rule: under a closed binding, a catalog that is neither an
ancestor nor a descendant of a cited catalog stays `catalog_fork`; an old-key signer can create
such a same-revision sibling after the resolution (indistinguishable from a pre-existing
uncited fork), which the closing rule does not end. The pinned test (one-sided citation stays a
fork) is kept as specified.

Non-claims (4a): no route switch or route output; no retained-client acceptance until 4c; no
native `catalog_eval`; no custodian closed-binding refusal; C10–C15 stay OPEN (4a asserts C10
authority acceptance and node fields only); no Core primitive, wire atom or quarantine reason;
historical vectors (`replacement_v1.json`, `ts_trust.json`) and wire bytes unchanged; disposable
keys only. The convergence property file checks D-279 verdict monotonicity (property (a)) over
its scenario pools only, on BEAM; not written in 4a: the D-279 StreamData property over
interleavings of revokes, transfers, beacons, rotations and replacements, and the C13
redundant-late-beacon case (PR 4b, row 380), nor the row 389 convergence evidence.
Reason
The evidence and design history belongs in the PR and this log. The plan is still about 450 lines: the excess over 400 predates PR #221 (main is 420) and lies in the normative PR 4 test list that 4b and 4c still use, so trimming it is left to a plan-only change.

D-641 PR 4a convergence amendment, revision 2 (rows 386, 387, 389)

When
2026-11-16, #221
Context
The coordinator checked the eight critique findings on revision 1 against 179fee056 and accepted all of them. The operator rule is that a forked or changed authority freezes routes and never falls back to the old signer.
Decision
Adopted, amending the convergence design (D-588 to D-639) only in the named clauses. Row 386 repair (a): trust_pending carries freeze = the complete-part decision's freezing block when it does not accept (C1) and serving = Serving_c(A); advance/2 ORs freeze into the sticky bit and the :unchanged sentinel is removed. C6: changed? is not masked by a missing A.catalog when A.binding is refused or closed out. C4: the authoring escape also covers chain-pending catalogs bound to a claim parent or a dropped rotation (the stated D-A18 asymmetry, S16g). C3: TS reserves a route with the non-nullish sentinel RESERVED, every lookup is has-based and JSON carries null. Row 387: conflicting cited route maps reserve the route to none instead of failing the floor. C5: the pending set and escape set are to come from an independent reference model (designed in section 3.2, not yet built; row 389). C2, C7, C8: the row 381 wiring obligation, the B1c omission companion, and the differential mapping row of D-642.
Reason
Rule 3: the revision-1 text claimed that a pending page freezes exactly as a complete delivery would, which C1 showed false; the other findings were slips in the mechanism or the ledger. Repair (a) with the C1 freeze rule awaits operator confirmation (row 388).

D-645 Amendment build review round 1: finding 3, floor joins are memoized and deduplicated

When
2026-11-16, #221
Context
Finding 3 (P2): floor joins concatenated duplicate entries and floor_of was unmemoized, so a lattice of nil citations grew floors exponentially (2,048 copies per head at 24 ops).
Decision
Fixed in both runtimes: floor_of is memoized per node id, and the join keeps the first copy of each whole entry in order (TS keys on the explicit CatalogEntry fields). Deduplication is never by replica, which would settle the entry-variant class by first-wins. F3: a 12-level vector scenario plus a BEAM and a TS floor-size test, each red with deduplication off; a 30-level BEAM test, which times out with the memo off.
Reason
Extends-checking reads each floor entry independently, so dropping identical copies changes no verdict. The floor is bounded by the distinct entries of the held catalogs.

D-649 PR 4a amendment design: Row 386: trust_pending carries a derived freeze bit (repair (a))

When
2026-11-16, #221
Context
Row 386 P1 x2; D-633 listed options (a), (b) and (c). Flagged for operator confirmation (row 388, question 4).
Options
(a) sticky derived freeze bit (chosen); (b) serve nil without freezing; (c) keep (violates lifecycle section 4)
Decision
trust_pending returns freeze and serving, computed from complete authenticated evidence only. advance ORs freeze into frozen and serves nil while frozen. Only an accepting decision that clears can lift it.
Reason
This is the coordinator's choice under the operator rule. Without it, an unrelated withheld page keeps routes alive under a refused binding (repros 1 and 2).

D-650 PR 4a amendment design: Complete evidence defined by the existing classification

When
2026-11-16, #221
Context
The lemma covers closed_out_pending, closed_status with a pending chain, and catalog candidates refuted early.
Decision
The bit reads Core verdicts on complete ops, nodes and catalog verdicts out of pending, and the classification built from them. It reuses the resolver's own changed? and serving/4.
Reason
Pending artifacts are never nodes or citations. The three paths that name pending artifacts classify only those artifacts, so the result equals the complete part for the watermark.

D-651 PR 4a amendment design: A watermark catalog with a pending chain is missing, not changed

When
2026-11-16, #221
Context
closed_status maps an uncited pending catalog to closed_out.
Decision
accepted_catalog_status returns missing when the watermark catalog's own verdict is pending or binding_pending, on both paths.
Reason
Otherwise a withheld previous of the watermark catalog under a closed binding would read as closed out and freeze from pending evidence.

D-652 PR 4a amendment design: A closed watermark binding serves nil on trust_pending without freezing

When
2026-11-16, #221
Context
Goes beyond repair (a) as worded.
Options
Freeze also on closed (rejected: it contradicts D-620's derived reading); serve unchanged (rejected: old-signer fallback)
Decision
trust_pending uses the same serving rule as the ok path: nil when the watermark binding is closed, changed or forked on complete evidence, with no freeze unless it changed.
Reason
Otherwise a withheld page keeps serving under a binding an honored replacement has closed. This extends D-600, which is still unconfirmed, so it is added to row 388 (question 5).

D-653 PR 4a amendment design: The unchanged serving sentinel is removed

When
2026-11-16, #221
Context
S16c is red only in this encoding, and that is reported.
Decision
advance returns the watermark catalog or nil on trust_pending, never unchanged.
Reason
Only the watermark is ever served without promotion. HEAD also returned unchanged while frozen, which contradicted 'nothing is served while frozen'.

D-654 PR 4a amendment design: Authoring escapes trust_pending for rotations the claim closes out

When
2026-11-16, #221
Context
Same set as D-A16 pending_out? once the parents are closed; mirrors the D-614 head-limit escape. Widened to the claim's D-275 set by D-644.
Decision
resolve_for_claim drops pending rotations whose rotation-parent chain reaches a claim parent, their descendants and their catalogs, but only when every pending id is of that kind. The post-sign check re-resolves the full input.
Reason
Without it, repro 2's sticky freeze could never lift: the lifting resolution could not be prepared while the old signer withholds the prior.

D-655 PR 4a amendment design: No liveness under a permanently withheld dependency

When
2026-11-16, #221
Context
Retention and bounded fetch belong to 4c.
Decision
Nothing is accepted while resolve is trust_pending. A permanently withheld dependency of a held op keeps the client unpromoted, and frozen if a change is shown.
Reason
This is the D-A11 trade that D-633 named. The amendment changes only what is served meanwhile, not whether partial promotion is allowed.

D-656 PR 4a amendment design: Row 387: a conflicting floor route becomes reserved to none

When
2026-11-16, #221
Context
Row 387 P1 x2; D-634 proposal verified against both repros.
Options
Reserved to none (chosen); union-level cross-branch classification (rejected: non-monotone); keep :bad (permanent freeze)
Decision
Joining floors maps a route to none when cited priors map it to different replicas. No catalog in that lineage may use it. Replicas may move to fresh routes. merge_floor no longer fails on routes.
Reason
It is intrinsic, so D-279 holds. It matches lifecycle section 2 ('moving A from /r/X to /r/Y never frees /r/X for B'). It makes the resolution citing route-conflicting siblings promotable, including siblings signed by a revoked key.

D-657 PR 4a amendment design: One floor admission check for verifier and authoring

When
2026-11-16, #221
Context
carries? runs before held_conflict?, so D-635 cannot bypass the reservation.
Decision
The graph exports floors with route maps, plus catalog_routes. carries? becomes the same Admit check first_catalog uses, failing closed on a missing floor.
Reason
HEAD read a :bad floor as an empty list, and the carried-pair exemption prepared entries on routes the verifier refuses.

D-658 PR 4a amendment design: Entry-variant and overflow floors stay preconditions and get rows

When
2026-11-16, #221
Context
Sibling classes the earlier liveness argument still misses.
Options
Variant-tolerant floor (offered to the operator); keep as precondition (chosen for now)
Decision
Precondition P8 is added: cited priors agree on each replica's extended keys, and the union fits the 13-entry limit with at most 12 Threads. Two P2 rows are filed (rows 390 and 391). The floor rule is not widened.
Reason
Both are forgeable by the old catalog key in 4a, because the graph has no entry facts. Choosing a surviving variant or dropping a Thread touches section 2 immutability and retirement (D-616), which is an operator question under rule 3.

D-659 PR 4a amendment design: Property (b) uses an independent reference model

When
2026-11-16, #221
Context
Narrows D-636, which allowed union classification. Not built in this PR; row 389 (D-647).
Decision
A test-side model recomputes heads, closure, catalog classes, Changed, Serving, Seen, Carry, RouteCompat and R* from artifact facts, intrinsic verdicts and Core refusals. It never reads decision fields or union classification.
Reason
The existing oracle read d.accepted, d.head and d.catalog, so it could not catch errors in the decide layer.

Property (b) requires cover_claim ok on R* and the verifier accepting R* as the sole head with no fork, from every reachable state where its preconditions hold.

Row 387 was exactly a disagreement between the two: cover_claim prepared what the verifier refused.

D-661 PR 4a amendment design: Pending soundness property (h)

D-662 PR 4a amendment design: Generator and run counts

D-663 PR 4a amendment design: Property (f) scope

When
2026-11-16, #221
Context
Built as five deterministic sequences; the StreamData generator is row 389.
Decision
The CatalogTrust differential compares accepted watermark, served or not, and reason class on replacement-free histories. It excludes entry facts, cross-branch route reuse, security_block and the persisted block, and its docstring says it cannot exercise freeze.
Reason
Replacement-free histories have no closed set or refused node. security_block stays in catalog_trust after 4c (D-616).

D-666 PR 4a amendment design: C1: trust_pending freeze comes from the complete-part decision; unanchored pending is a 4c eviction obligation

When
2026-11-16, #221
Context

With revision 1's freeze=Changed_c(A), delivering T2+R2+K2 plus an unresolvable O freezes, while the same delivery without O accepts R2. That makes the claim in 1.5 false.
Options
(a) freeze=Changed_c(A) (revision 1); (b) freeze = the complete-part decision's block.freeze when it is not accepting; (c) (b) plus 4c eviction of unanchored pending artifacts
Decision
Accept (c). detail.freeze = not D_c.accepted and D_c.block.freeze. Serving stays Serving_c, so it is nil whenever A is changed. The pending arm still only ORs into frozen. 4c (rows 381/388) evicts unanchored pending artifacts after bounded fetch. The keyless unknown-binding catalog is named explicitly in 1.7.
Reason
This makes the freeze equal to what a complete delivery of E(U) would set, so the statement in 1.5 becomes true. It keeps no-unfreeze-from-pending and no-serve-under-change. A pending id that cannot be removed is a stall regardless of the freeze bit, and the resolver cannot drop it without trusting an absence, so the fix belongs in intake. Pinned by S16h, (b4) and M12.

D-668 PR 4a amendment design: C3: the TS reserved route uses a non-nullish sentinel and has-based lookups

D-669 PR 4a amendment design: C4: the authoring escape also drops chain-pending catalogs bound to a claim parent

When
2026-11-16, #221
Context
Verified: catalog_status under an open, non-refused binding returns {:pending} for v == :pending, and closed_status maps an uncited chain-pending catalog to :closed_out once the binding is closed, so post-sign passes.
Decision
Accept. drop_pending_under also drops intrinsic :pending catalogs whose binding is a claim parent or a dropped rotation. :binding_pending catalogs are not dropped, because post-sign would still pend them. The asymmetry (a later-arriving real previous can turn the catalog :uncovered) is stated and pinned in S16g.
Reason
It is the same closed-out-after-claim class that 1.6 already relies on, and the post-sign re-resolve checks it independently. Without it, a compromised catalog key stalls its own replacement unless the operator applies the D-A18 omission.

D-671 PR 4a amendment design: C6: a refused or closed-out accepted binding freezes even when its catalog is :missing

When
2026-11-16, #221
Context
Verified: decide/5 :1348 has changed? = a != nil and not missing? and ..., and the 1.1 case-2 amendment makes acs :missing for a pending A.catalog, so complete evidence of R1 refused does not freeze while K1's previous is withheld.
Options

Decision

Any binding_graph vector row whose freeze flips is reported, and the operator is asked to confirm in row 388 (question 7).
Reason
The operator rule says a changed authority freezes routes. A refused accepted binding is a changed authority whatever the state of its catalog's chain. Rewording would keep a gap that conflicts with the settled rule. The binding_graph vectors are new in this PR, so no historical bytes change. Pinned by S16i and M14.

D-685 Row 388: nine questions answered under delegation

When
2026-11-16, #221
Context
Row 388 held nine open questions from the PR 4a convergence design. Each is answered with the as-built, fail-closed default.
Options
Each answer keeps what is built and keeps it fail-closed. The text of design section 1.7 is not in the repository; the reading of q6 comes from D-666, D-617 and D-A16 in D-640.
Decision
(1) Confirm: an honored replacement head that closes the accepted binding but is not yet promotable serves nil with no block. (2) Keep the built rule: authoring `cover_claim` requires every closed binding that holds an uncovered catalog as an extra parent, with that catalog as a prior. (3) Accept the precondition: no liveness is claimed when an accepted entry's creation or reference target is Core-refused (P7). (4) Same as row 386: repair (a) (D-684). (5) Confirm: a closed but unchanged watermark serves nil under `trust_pending`. (6) Accept the section 1.7 trade. In plain words: a withheld or unresolvable page can stall a client, because the resolver will not trust an absence. A stalled client serves nil or stays frozen. It never serves under a refused binding. The keyless unanchored case is one such stall: a catalog whose binding no held artifact names stays pending until 4c evicts unanchored pending artifacts after a bounded fetch. Until then an attacker can leave one pending, so a stall is possible; nothing is served wrongly (D-666). (7) Confirm: a refused or closed-out binding with a missing catalog freezes (C6). (8) Keep the C7 omission dependency (B1c). In plain words: an old-key catalog with a bad chain under a refused binding blocks authoring `prepare` unless the admin leaves it out of the claim. Post-sign passes when it is held. We accept that the admin must omit it by hand, and we do not add an automatic exemption (D-672). (9) Superseded for history-inconsistent variants by the entry-facts fix (repair (c), commits 390eb5e35, 6bae1420a, 8a65e9ffd, 8a01dc414). The history-consistent remainder is the open liveness row 392.
Reason
ASSUMED UNDER DELEGATION; reversible. Row 388 is DONE on that basis.
