D-688 Row 316: the replacement path is wired by composition in new files

When
2026-11-16, stacked on #221
Context
The operator goal is that a client which already trusts a Treehouse catalog accepts a replacement catalog signed by an authorized admin, without the old catalog signer's key, and that the operator can see it run. Row 381 says to hook the replacement graph into catalog_trust.ex and treehouse_catalog_trust.ts. Thirteen live Codex branches hold unmerged edits to those files.
Decision
Under the operator's 2026-11-16 delegation (D-683), wire it by composition in new files instead: Treehouse.CatalogReplacementTrust and its TS twin call CatalogTrust.evaluate for the replacement-free part, keep replacement-bound catalogs in a sidecar, and use CatalogBindingGraph.resolve and advance/2 for what is accepted and served once the Space history holds a replace_catalog_v1 op. The graph sees only sidecar catalogs bound to a held replacement op, so a forged catalog bound to an unheld id waits in the sidecar and cannot pin the client (probed: before this filter it did). A runnable demo (scripts/treehouse_replacement_demo.sh) replays the same pages in BEAM and TS. No existing file under the hard exclusion was edited. This is a new-files demonstration, not PR 4c, so the 267/269 STOP gate (plan 316) is not crossed. Row 381 stays open for the internal hook and for deleting the duplicated chain logic. ASSUMED UNDER DELEGATION; reversible: delete the new files.
Not claimed
No production caller: no shipped client path evaluates a catalog and dials its routes today (resolve_route has no caller outside tests), and nothing is dialled. C10 proper needs an R04 successor with a nil parent; the demo uses a leased admin, so C10 to C15 stay OPEN. Native catalog_eval is untouched. The sidecar has no byte budget (row 355) and no saved-state validation (row 395). A rotation under a replacement is refused with trust_pending (row 395). An incomplete Space closure serves nothing new. An honored replacement with no held first catalog serves nothing, by D-275 and row 388 answer (1).
Reason
Waiting for the Codex branches to merge would leave the operator unable to see the feature run. Composition keeps every historical vector and wire byte unchanged and adds no Core primitive, wire atom or quarantine reason.

D-689 Row 316: Codex review of the composed evaluator; a replace-shaped op never widens what is served

When
2026-11-16, stacked on #221
Context
Codex review of Treehouse.CatalogReplacementTrust and its TS twin, Sol 6.1 at xhigh effort. The first attempt was refused by the provider's content filter. The second, with neutral wording, returned one P1 and two P2 findings, each reproduced in BEAM and TS. P1: a malformed replacement command (an outsider-signed {:replace_catalog_v1, []} with no capability) bypassed an authority freeze, because its presence activated composition, which ignored the trust block and restored both routes. P2: a forged signature variant of an authentic catalog overwrote it in the graph input, which is keyed by the signature-free catalog id, so the wrapper froze and the authentic catalog did not recover. P2: public wrap dropped the accepted watermark of a valid trust state, so a replacement that omitted an accepted Thread was accepted instead of freezing.
Decision
All three are fixed in both runtimes, each with a failing test first. Rule adopted for the P1 class: the mere presence of a replacement-shaped op never widens what is served. Composition engages only when the resolver treats an op as a replacement node or as raw refusal evidence, and a malformed or unauthorized op that is neither changes nothing. While CatalogTrust is blocked, its decision stands and nothing is served, except that a replacement the graph honors, whose first catalog it accepts with the serving node bound to that replacement, supersedes the block. Routes under the bootstrap or a rotation binding are never served while blocked, and the graph's watermark on a replacement binding is never overwritten by the trust state's accepted catalog. An old-key catalog fork still does not stop an honest R1 and K1. P2 forged variant: a sidecar catalog bound to a held replacement op is admitted only if its signature verifies against that op's claimed catalog key, once per catalog id, and an unverifiable variant is dropped. P2 wrap: wrap carries the accepted watermark and the cumulative route table of the accepted catalog. Pins: a table test over a blocked trust state and {no op, malformed op, unauthorized op, honored op without its catalog, honored op with its accepted catalog}; three forged-variant tests; two wrap tests; and four extra runs in the demo that BEAM and TS must agree on.
Not claimed
There was no confirming Codex round after the fixes. The unauthorized-op row of the table is pinned under a fork block, not an authority block. A catalog bound to an op that is not yet held waits unverified, so a forged variant is dropped only when its op arrives.
Reason
Fixing the class, not the instance, keeps the composed path from ever being more permissive than the block it wraps. ASSUMED UNDER DELEGATION; reversible.

D-690 PR 4a CI: browser sources, and default-suite property runs cut with deep runs behind a tag

When
2026-11-16, #221 and #223
Context
On head 48cd417f0 the browser-proof job failed mix compile --warnings-as-errors: apps/lattice_popcorn_spike/scripts/shared.mjs copies an explicit list of modules and lacked treehouse/catalog_entries.ex, which the binding graph calls, and the first merge_floor/2 clause was unreachable because floor_of/3 stops folding once the accumulator is :bad. Locally the three files fell from about 1117 to about 93 seconds. The job timeout is unchanged.
Not claimed
Playwright on the new runner image is not fixed here (row 398). The deep runs are not in CI. The measured saving is local; the hosted step time after this change is not yet known.
Reason
The shallow default keeps the safety properties and every deterministic scenario in CI while the deep sampling stays one command away. ASSUMED UNDER DELEGATION; reversible.

D-730 Row 167: divergence explainer decisions (operator delegation 2026-11-16)

When
2026-11-16, branch claude/167-divergence-explainer, checked against origin/main 4ee13d7d6
Context
Plan 167 was reconciled at 91bb6ca6; authority.ex and the carrier tests have since changed. The brief resolved several plan inconsistencies.
Decision
(1) Township.Divergence.explain/2 is the only public function; mismatched replicas return %{error: :different_replicas, a:, b:} (a map, not an error tuple). (2) Content privacy: state mismatch values carry only a length or entry count and an 8 character sha256 prefix, never participant content. (3) Holder fingerprints are a 12 character sha256 prefix of the public key (not Identity.fingerprint/1). (4) quarantine_delta (Authority.analyze reasons) and structural_delta (Log.quarantine) are never merged. (5) If state bytes differ but no field differs, an :unattributed sentinel entry is emitted; the byte oracle stays authoritative and converged? additionally requires all deltas empty. (6) The mix task loads both bundles through AuditBundle.load_verified/1 and refuses unverified or different-replica bundles. (7) Plan Step 6 (enrich carrier test failure messages) is not done: those tests mostly hold no Log, so it is filed as row 420. (8) No substrate change, no new Core primitive, wire atom or quarantine reason.
Reason
Most conservative reading of the brief; a diagnostic that cannot weaken any existing assertion. Rows 420 and 421 are open.

D-731 Row 167: review round 1 fixes

When
2026-11-16, branch claude/167-divergence-explainer
Context
Review found that log-derived ids and labels were printed raw (a forged op id could emit a fake verdict line or terminal controls, and invalid UTF-8 crashed --format json), that sets and lists with equal elements were normalized to the same value, and that the completeness property did not check attribution.
Decision
(1) The mix task prints ids and replica labels verbatim only when they match a plain token pattern, otherwise as an escaped Elixir literal; JSON output escapes non-UTF-8 binaries the same way. (2) A MapSet is compared as a tagged sorted set, so it never equals a list. (3) State keys outside the declared five fields are also compared, in sorted order, after the declared ones. (4) The property now asserts the exact set of differing state keys and refutes :unattributed. No wire, vector or authority change. All three P2 findings reproduced with failing tests first.
Reason
Smallest change that makes the diagnostic safe to print and unable to hide a difference. Row 421 is narrowed: extra keys are now attributed, only a public field list test remains.

D-732 Row 167: review round 2 fixes

When
2026-11-16, branch claude/167-divergence-explainer
Context
Round 2 found that a state value shaped like the internal set marker ({:set, :oops}) crashed the summary, that normalizing sets to a tagged tuple still equated a MapSet with a lookalike tuple, that verification errors were joined raw into the Mix error (forged verdict line and escape codes), that JSON rendered distinct invalid-UTF-8 ids as the same string, and that the escaping test did not split messages into physical lines.
Decision
(1) No normalization: state values are compared with strict equality and a MapSet is summarized from its own struct bytes. (2) Verification errors and the bundle path are escaped (inspect) before they reach Mix.raise. (3) Invalid UTF-8 binaries in JSON become {"bytes_base64": ...}; valid strings are unchanged. (4) The drain helper splits messages on newlines and a new test covers verification-error injection. All three P2 findings reproduced with failing tests first; both P3 findings fixed. No wire, vector or authority change.
Reason
Comparing raw values removes the class of normalization collisions instead of patching one marker, and a tagged object is the smallest lossless JSON encoding.

D-794 Row 156: rendering rules that keep the report verbatim

When

Status

D-820 Township.ReadModel text slots: valid UTF-8 or the fixed marker "(malformed)"

When
2026-11-16, row 325, #230; decided under operator delegation (2026-11-16), reversible
Context
Row 325 (row 311 residual, D-316). Township.ReadModel copied those terms, and the denied admit/remove_member member term, verbatim into the model, the replay frames and AuditBundle state.json. The LiveView then raised in Jason.encode!(@causal_replay) or Phoenix.HTML.Safe, and AuditBundle.write raised.
Decision
Follow the Lattice.Graph.AuthorityBody display-safety contract (row 311, merged on main as b8d14391f) and state it locally in the Township.ReadModel moduledoc. threads.title, threads.summary, each threads.posts entry, each members.current entry, each members.denied[].member and the matching replay-frame state slots are valid UTF-8. The helper stays private to Township, so a later row 324 Toolshed marker choice is not coupled to it. Non-claims: no length bound, no control-character or bidi filtering, and the marker is not proof of malformation because a member may post that literal text (structural flag filed as row 480). The replay/1 doc no longer says JSON-safe without qualification, and the denied_member_mutation type now says member: String.t().
Reason
Replacing rather than inspecting is the most conservative choice: the contract allows either, inspect/1 output would show attacker-chosen structure in the civic panel, and the fixed marker keeps model, replay and bundle bytes unchanged on honest logs. No public API, wire atom, quarantine reason or Core primitive changes.

D-760 Row 313: defer authority audit writes; fail closed only when the backlog is full

When
2026-11-16, row 313 (branch claude/313-capstore-topology-audit-defer)
Context
Decided under operator delegation (2026-11-16), reversible. CapStore and Topology called Audit.record/2 inside handle_call and hold caps and tabs only in memory, so an Audit restart exited them and wiped every cap and tab. LatticeCore.Supervisor is one_for_one with the default restart intensity, so repeated exits can take the core app down.
Decision
Options: fail every authority call closed while Audit is down; defer every record without bound; defer into a bounded per-process backlog and refuse only authority-increasing actions when it is full (chosen). Decision: each process keeps one Lattice.Audit.Backlog (default limit 10_000, :authority_audit_backlog_limit). Revocations, revoke_tab, denials, connects, worker registration, disconnects, ejects, connection-down closes, worker cleanup and bridge cleanup always commit and defer. grant, delegate, a successful authorize (:cap_use) and Topology.create_bridge/3 flush first and reply {:error, :audit_unavailable}, state unchanged, while the backlog is still full.
Reason
Revocation must never wait on Audit, and a committed authority increase always has its record written or queued, so no grant goes unrecorded. Normal restarts are absorbed. Non-claims: a stalled Audit still exits CapStore and Topology (D-238); nothing is durable; Audit's own history still does not survive its restart; there is no global Audit order across CapStore, Topology and LiveOps.

D-761 One pure Backlog module; timers stay in each owner

When
2026-11-16, row 313 (branch claude/313-capstore-topology-audit-defer)
Context
Decided under operator delegation (2026-11-16), reversible. CapStore and Topology need the same queue, gap and flush rules that LiveOps has (D-240, D-249, D-253).
Decision
New Lattice.Audit.Backlog: a struct with new/1, record/3, full?/1, flush/2 (allowance 500), drain/1, discard_into_gap/2, status/1, owed?/1 and retry_ms/0 (:authority_audit_retry_ms, default 1_000, read at runtime as in D-257). It never arms timers or reads the mailbox. Each owner keeps audit_retry and arms one :flush_audit timer while records are owed: 0 ms after a full batch, the retry interval when Audit was unavailable, and a stale delivered message is cleared on cancel (D-242, D-250, D-253). LiveOps is unchanged.
Reason
Keeps the process logic small and lets the rules be tested once. Credo DuplicatedCode is disabled, so the few timer lines repeated in the two owners are left in place rather than hidden in a helper process.

D-762 Deferred record markers and the :audit_gap record

When
2026-11-16, row 313 (branch claude/313-capstore-topology-audit-defer)
Context
Decided under operator delegation (2026-11-16), reversible. A replayed record lands at replay time and interleaves with other writers.
Decision
A queued record is replayed with its original metadata plus audit_deferred: true, audit_source: module and audit_seq: n, a per-source counter assigned at enqueue. Records written on time are byte-identical to before. Dropped records and records discarded by reset are counted per reason and written as :audit_gap %{source: module, dropped: n, reason: :backlog_full | :reset} before any queued record; queued records wait while any gap is unwritten (D-249).
Reason
An evidence reader can tell a record was late, which process wrote it and its order within that process. audit_seq does not order records across processes.

D-763 Caller-side CapStore denials share the backlog

When
2026-11-16, row 313 (branch claude/313-capstore-topology-audit-defer)
Context
Decided under operator delegation (2026-11-16), reversible. Five CapStore writes ran in the caller's process (delegate with nil delegator at two sites, delegate, revoke and authorize with a malformed token), so they exited the caller during an Audit restart.
Decision
They now call GenServer.call(CapStore, {:record_deny, metadata}) with unchanged type and metadata, and the caller still gets its original error.
Reason
One writer per source keeps audit_seq meaningful. Cost: these denials now need CapStore to be running, which every other CapStore path already does.

D-764 Where the fail-closed check sits and how a refusal is counted

When
2026-11-16, row 313 (branch claude/313-capstore-topology-audit-defer)
Context
Decided under operator delegation (2026-11-16), reversible. A refused action must not leave partial state, such as a cap id in Topology's issued_caps.
Decision
grant: after the connected and collision checks, before Topology.register_cap/2. delegate: immediately before register_cap. authorize: after check_cap succeeds, before uses and sessions are stored. create_bridge: after both tabs match. A refusal records a :deny with reason :audit_unavailable through the backlog; because the queue is full it is dropped and adds 1 to the :backlog_full gap. Gateway passes the new error through; web_socket public_error/1 maps unknown reasons to "error", and no wire atom or quarantine reason is added.
Reason
The refusal is counted, not silent, and it leaves caps, uses, sessions, tabs and bridges untouched.

D-765 Reset drains, then gaps the remainder and rebuilds the backlog

When
2026-11-16, row 313 (branch claude/313-capstore-topology-audit-defer)
Context
Decided under operator delegation (2026-11-16), reversible. Lattice.reset!/0 calls Topology.reset/0 and CapStore.reset/0 in test setup, so reset must not be refused (D-241).
Decision
reset cancels the timer, drains (bounded by the queue limit plus the gap records, which avoids the row 314 unbounded drain), discards what is left into the :reset gap, and returns a backlog built by new/1 that carries the gaps and the audit_seq counter. Topology writes its worker cleanup records before the drain. A changed limit env takes effect at that reset.
Reason
The loss is visible as an :audit_gap record, and a test limit does not leak once a later setup resets.

D-766 Every recording handler flushes first when records are owed

When
2026-11-16, row 313 (branch claude/313-capstore-topology-audit-defer)
Context
Decided under operator delegation (2026-11-16), reversible. The brief named the four authority-increasing actions for the flush; revocations and denials would otherwise queue behind the backlog until the timer fired.
Decision
Every CapStore and Topology handler that writes a record runs one bounded flush first when anything is owed. Read handlers (get, snapshot, tab_connected?, delivery_info, bridge_allowed?) never flush.
Reason
The next authority call after Audit returns replays the backlog before its own record. While Audit is down, the extra try_record finds no registered process and returns at once.

D-770 A refused Lattice.bridge revokes its cap; the id stays reserved

When
2026-11-16, row 313 review round 1 (branch claude/313-capstore-topology-audit-defer)
Context
Decided under operator delegation (2026-11-16), reversible. Finding 2 (P2, reproduced): Lattice.bridge/4 grants the bridge cap and then calls Topology.create_bridge/3. When Topology refused (:audit_unavailable on a full backlog, or the older :tab_not_connected), the cap stayed live and registered to the source tab, and a retry with the same explicit id failed with :cap_id_collision. Options: revoke on refusal, delete the cap record, or move the bridge into one CapStore call.
Decision
On any refusal, Lattice.bridge/4 revokes the cap with reason :bridge_refused and returns the refusal. The revoked id stays reserved, so a retry after Audit returns names a fresh id (default ids are random, so only explicit id: callers see this). Row 442 is done; an atomic grant-and-bridge call that would let a same-id retry succeed is row 446.
Reason
Most conservative under delegation: revocation always commits, even on a full backlog, and keeps the audit trail coherent (the deferred :grant is followed by a :revoke). Deleting the record would leave an audited grant with no cap, and reusing a revoked id would let one audited id name two caps. A single CapStore bridge call is a new API, beyond this slice. Tests: a refused bridge on a full Topology backlog and to a disconnected tab, both through Lattice.bridge/4.

D-771 LiveOps handles a refused grant and rolls back a partial registration

When
2026-11-16, row 313 review round 1 (branch claude/313-capstore-topology-audit-defer)
Context
Decided under operator delegation (2026-11-16), reversible. Finding 1 (P2, reproduced): with CapStore's audit backlog full, LiveOps.register_tab/1 failed its {:ok, cap} = Lattice.grant(...) match and LiveOps died, losing its state. The same match sat in the device and approval grants.
Decision
The role, device and publish grant helpers return the refusal. register_tab/1 stops at the first refusal, stops the devices it spawned, revokes the caps it granted, drops the actor and restores the role count, then returns {:error, :audit_unavailable}; a later register_tab/1 call after Audit returns registers afresh. Row 441 is done. approve_publish returns the refusal and leaves the approval pending. The WebSocket transport already ignores the result of register_tab/1, so the tab connects without LiveOps caps (fail closed) and nothing retries the registration; its disconnect cleanup already ignores {:error, :unknown_actor}. No transport change; a transport retry or refusal message is row 446.
Reason
A whole-or-nothing registration avoids an actor holding part of its role, and keeps LiveOps alive. Tests: a full backlog refusing an observer; a producer refused at the third role cap and at the device cap (caps revoked, device stopped, retry succeeds); a refused approval.

D-772 Row 313 review: two adversarial rounds and dispositions

When
2026-11-16, row 313 review rounds 1 and 2 (branch claude/313-capstore-topology-audit-defer)
Context
Decided under operator delegation (2026-11-16), reversible. Two independent adversarial reviews, as row 313 and D-244 ask (see D-767).
Decision
Round 1: CHANGES, 2 P2, 0 P3. (1) P2 cap_store.ex:148, a full CapStore backlog crashed LiveOps through the {:ok, cap} match in register_tab. Fixed: reproduced with a red test first; the grant helpers return the refusal, register_tab rolls back and returns {:error, :audit_unavailable}, approve_publish leaves the approval pending (D-771, row 441 done). The transport still ignores the result, so the tab connects with no LiveOps caps (fail closed); filed as row 446(b). (2) P2 topology.ex:160, a refused bridge left a live cap. Fixed: Lattice.bridge/4 revokes the cap with reason :bridge_refused (D-770, row 442 done). Only partly closed: the revoked id stays reserved, so a same-id retry returns :cap_id_collision; filed as row 446(a). Round 2: CHANGES, 0 P1 or P2, 2 P3. It verified both round 1 fixes. Its two P3s are left open, not fixed in this PR: (a) backlog.ex:133, drain/1 flushes every batch inside one reset handler, so with a large configured limit (100_000) reset and a queued call can pass their 5 s timeout, which repeats row 314 despite D-765 (row 447); (b) lattice_authority_audit_outage_test.exs:413, no test covers Topology's idle replay, so a no-op Topology retry timer passes all tests (row 448).
Reason
The last round found no P1 or P2, and it confirmed the round 1 fixes. The two final-round P3s are recorded as open rows. Nothing here claims durability, a stalled-Audit policy, a global Audit order, or a bounded reset at a non-default limit.
